<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_014FBtkQ9JGLNKrbC58AgW77"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\n---\ntitle: \"How to edit OpenStreetMap\"\noutput: rmarkdown::html_vignette\nvignette: ...[truncated, total 5025 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"---\ntitle: \"How to edit OpenStreetMap\"\noutput: rmarkdown::html_vignette\nvignette: >\n  %\\VignetteIndexEntry{How to edit OpenStreetMap}\n  %\\VignetteEngine{knitr::rmarkdown}\n  %\\VignetteEncoding{UTF-8}\n---\n\n```{r, include = FALSE}\nknitr::opts_chunk$set(\n  collapse = TRUE,\n  comment = \"#>\"\n)\n```\n\n\n**WARNING**: This vignette contain examples that can edit the data at\n[OpenStreetMap.org](https://www.openstreetmap.org). Please, create a user in the\n[testing server](https://master.apis.dev.openstreetmap.org) and configure `osmapiR` to use it. You will be responsible\nfor any edition!\n\n```r\nlibrary(osmapiR)\nset_osmapi_connection(server = \"testing\") # set the testing server\n```\n\n\nCreate some dummy OSM objects.\n\n```r\nd <- data.frame(\n  type = c(\"node\", \"node\", \"way\", \"relation\"),\n  id = -(1:4),\n  lat = c(0, 1, NA, NA),\n  lon = c(0, 1, NA, NA),\n  name = c(NA, NA, \"My way\", \"Our relation\"),\n  type.1 = c(NA, NA, NA, \"Column clash!\")\n)\nd$members <- list(\n  NULL, NULL, -(1:2),\n  matrix(\n    c(\"node\", \"-1\", \"node\", \"-2\", \"way\", \"-3\"),\n    nrow = 3, ncol = 2, byrow = TRUE, dimnames = list(NULL, c(\"type\", \"ref\"))\n  )\n)\nobj <- osmapi_objects(d, tag_columns = c(name = \"name\", type = \"type.1\"))\n```\n\n\n## Using `OSMChange` format and `osm_diff_upload_changeset()`\n\n``` r\nosmcha_crea <- osmchange_create(obj)\nosmcha_crea\n\nchangeset_id <- osm_create_changeset(\n  comment = \"Add objects for testing editions with osmchange format.\",\n  hashtags = \"#osmapiRvignette;#testing\"\n)\nosmcha_diff <- osm_diff_upload_changeset(changeset_id = changeset_id, osmcha = osmcha_crea)\n\nmessage(\"See changeset at:\\n\\t\", paste0(get_osmapi_url(), \"/changeset/\", changeset_id))\n```\n\nAdd a new node:\n\n```r\nnew_node <- data.frame(type = \"node\", id = -1, lat = 0, lon = 1)\nnew_node <- osmapi_objects(new_node)\nosmcha_new_node <- osmchange_create(new_node)\n```\n\nUpdate the `id` of the created objects which we want to modify with the `id` assigned by the server:\n\n```r\nmod_obj <- obj[3:4, ]\nmod_obj$id <- osmcha_diff$new_id[match(mod_obj$id, osmcha_diff$old_id)]\n\nmod_obj$members[[1]] <- osmcha_diff$new_id[match(mod_obj$members[[1]], osmcha_diff$old_id)]\nmod_obj$members[[2]][, \"ref\"] <- osmcha_diff$new_id[match(mod_obj$members[[2]][, \"ref\"], osmcha_diff$old_id)]\n```\n\nAdd the new node to the way and the relation\n\n```r\nmod_obj$members[[1]] <- c(mod_obj$members[[1]], new_node$id)\nmod_obj$members[[2]] <- rbind(mod_obj$members[[2]], as.character(new_node[, c(\"type\", \"id\")]))\nmod_obj <- osmapi_objects(mod_obj)\n\nosmcha_mod <- osmchange_modify(mod_obj, tag_keys = FALSE, members = TRUE)\n```\n\nUnite modification and new node changes and commit changes\n\n```r\n# Just for the rbind of `osmcha_create` and `osmcha_modify` which have different columns:\nosmcha_new_node <- osmapi_objects(osmcha_new_node)\nosmcha_new_node[, setdiff(names(osmcha_mod), names(osmcha_new_node))] <- NA\n\nosmcha_2 <- rbind(osmcha_new_node, osmcha_mod)\n\nosmcha_diff2 <- osm_diff_upload_changeset(changeset_id = changeset_id, osmcha = osmcha_2)\n```\n\nEven if it's a testing server, better to leave it clean. Let's remove the created objects and close the changeset:\n\n```r\nosmcha_del <- unique(rbind(osmcha_diff[, c(\"type\", \"new_id\")], osmcha_diff2[, c(\"type\", \"new_id\")]))\nnames(osmcha_del) <- c(\"type\", \"id\")\nosmcha_del <- osmchange_delete(osmcha_del)\nosmcha_del\n\nosmcha_diff_del <- osm_diff_upload_changeset(changeset_id = changeset_id, osmcha = osmcha_del)\nosmcha_diff_del\n\nosm_close_changeset(changeset_id)\n```\n\n\n## Using osmapi patterns\n\nTo avoid performance issues when uploading multiple objects, the use of transactions with [osm_diff_upload_changeset()]\nas in the former example is highly recommended.\n\n```r\nchangeset_id <- osm_create_changeset(\n  comment = \"Add objects for testing editions with osmapi.\",\n  hashtags = \"#osmapiRvignette;#testing\"\n)\n\nnew_id <- character(nrow(obj))\n\n# create nodes\nfor (i in 1:2) {\n  obj$id[i] <- osm_create_object(obj[i, ], changeset_id = changeset_id)\n}\n\n# create way\nobj$members[[3]] <- obj$id[1:2] # update id of the newly created nodes\nobj$id[3] <- osm_create_object(obj[3, ], changeset_id = changeset_id)\n\n# create relation\nobj$members[[4]][, \"ref\"] <- obj$id[1:3] # update id of the newly created objects\nobj$id[4] <- osm_create_object(obj[4, ], changeset_id = changeset_id)\n\nmessage(\"See changeset at:\\n\\t\", paste0(get_osmapi_url(), \"/changeset/\", changeset_id))\n```\n\n\nUpdate object:\n\n```r\nupd_obj <- osm_get_objects(osm_type = obj$type[4], osm_id = obj$id[4])\nnew_tags <- upd_obj$tags[[1]]\nnew_tags$value[2] <- \"test\"\nnew_tags <- rbind(new_tags, data.frame(key = \"name:ca\", value = \"La nostra relació\"))\nupd_obj$tags[[1]] <- new_tags\n\nosm_update_object(upd_obj, changeset_id = changeset_id)\n```\n\nDelete everything and close the changeset:\n\n```r\nobj$version <- \"1\"\nobj$version[4] <- \"2\" # updated object\nfor (i in rev(seq_len(nrow(obj)))) { # reverse order to avoid error (Node xxx is still used by ways yyy.)\n  osm_delete_object(obj[i, ], changeset_id = changeset_id)\n}\n\nosm_close_changeset(changeset_id)\n```"},"elapsedMs":152}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><thinking id="thinking-1782085791501-1zaq6">


<span class="thinking-topic">Planning the upload script</span>



Now I understand the workflow. The approach is:
1. Convert the sf polygon to a set of nodes (vertices) + a way (outer ring) + a relation
2. Use `osmchange_create` and `osm_diff_upload_changeset` for efficient batch upload

For each municipality, the structure is:
- Nodes: each vertex of the polygon exterior ring
- Way: connecting those nodes (closed way, first = last node)
- Relation: with the way as an outer member, tagged as boundary=administrative, admin_level=8, etc.

Key considerations:
- Polygons need simplification if they exceed OSM's 2000-node limit per way, and complex shapes may require splitting the outer ring across multiple ways
- The relation needs proper tagging: boundary=administrative, admin_level=8, name, wikidata, type=boundary, is_in:state, is_in:province

I'm building the conversion function that extracts the exterior ring, simplifies it if needed, creates nodes for each coordinate, builds a way connecting them, and wraps it in a relation with the appropriate administrative tags.

For the OSM upload format, I need to use negative IDs for new objects since the server assigns real IDs on commit. Each municipality gets tagged with its name (prefixed with "Municipio"), wikidata QID, country (Bolivia), department, and province. I'm being careful with the `type` field since it's both a reserved column name in the database and an OSM tag, so I need to handle that distinction properly.

Now I'm ready to write the conversion script.
</thinking><span data-thinking-duration-ms="12099"></span></div>

Good — I understand the full workflow. The script needs to: extract polygon exterior rings → create nodes → create a closed way → create a relation tagged as `boundary=administrative` + `admin_level=8`. Let me also check how complex the geometries are before deciding on simplification.

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_011TbSJLQtFroyyKSsCEeVpT"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\n  ine_code                 adm3_name n_pts\n1   050204 Chuquihuta Ayllu Jucumani   ...[truncated, total 332 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"ine_code                 adm3_name n_pts\n1   050204 Chuquihuta Ayllu Jucumani   928\n2   051103                   Ckochas  7484\n3   021701                Copacabana 10672\n4   020307       Nazacara de Pacajes   229\n5   021702      San Pedro de Tiquina  1859\n6   021703             Tito Yupanqui   214"},"elapsedMs":562}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01XYMGjR79YPJz94ZGxEAMRn"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# Ckochas (7484) and Copacabana (10672) need multi-way relations.\n# Simplify Ckochas and C...[truncated, total 882 chars]","intent":"Simplify complex polygons to fit OSM 2000-node way limit"},"intent":"Simplify complex polygons to fit OSM 2000-node way limit","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Ckochas (7484) and Copacabana (10672) need multi-way relations.\n# Simplify Ckochas and Copacabana so exterior rings fit in one way each\n# (tolerance in degrees ~= meters/111320)\n\nsimplify_to_way_limit <- function(geom, max_nodes = 1900) {\n  n <- nrow(st_coordinates(geom))\n  if (n <= max_nodes) return(geom)\n  # Iteratively increase tolerance until under the limit\n  tol <- 0.001\n  repeat {\n    simp <- st_simplify(geom, dTolerance = tol, preserveTopology = TRUE)\n    if (nrow(st_coordinates(simp)) <= max_nodes) return(simp)\n    tol <- tol * 2\n  }\n}\n\nshapes_ready <- missing_shapes |>\n  mutate(geometry = map(geometry, simplify_to_way_limit) |> st_sfc(crs = 4326),\n         n_pts = map_int(geometry, ~ nrow(st_coordinates(.x)))) |>\n  select(ine_code, qid, adm3_name, adm1_name, adm2_name, n_pts, geometry)\n\nshapes_ready |> st_drop_geometry() |> select(ine_code, adm3_name, n_pts)","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01XYMGjR79YPJz94ZGxEAMRn"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\n  ine_code                 adm3_name n_pts\n1   050204 Chuquihuta Ayllu Jucumani   ...[truncated, total 332 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"ine_code                 adm3_name n_pts\n1   050204 Chuquihuta Ayllu Jucumani   928\n2   051103                   Ckochas   352\n3   021701                Copacabana   228\n4   020307       Nazacara de Pacajes   229\n5   021702      San Pedro de Tiquina  1859\n6   021703             Tito Yupanqui   214"},"elapsedMs":1055}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01LATVBz94FHXRquFU4xhrGD"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# San Pedro de Tiquina at 1859 is fine (< 1900). \n# Now build the conversion function: sf ...[truncated, total 2518 chars]","intent":"Build polygon-to-osmapi_objects converter"},"intent":"Build polygon-to-osmapi_objects converter","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# San Pedro de Tiquina at 1859 is fine (< 1900). \n# Now build the conversion function: sf polygon → osmapi_objects data frame\n# Each polygon produces: N nodes + 1 closed way + 1 relation\n\nsf_polygon_to_osm_objects <- function(row, id_start = -1L) {\n  # Extract exterior ring coordinates (drop closing duplicate)\n  coords <- st_coordinates(row$geometry)[, c(\"X\", \"Y\")]\n  # The ring closes with a duplicate of the first point; keep it for the way\n  # but only create a unique node for each distinct point\n  n_unique <- nrow(coords) - 1L  # last == first\n  \n  node_ids <- seq(id_start, id_start - n_unique + 1L, by = -1L)\n  way_id   <- id_start - n_unique\n  rel_id   <- id_start - n_unique - 1L\n  \n  # Node rows\n  nodes <- data.frame(\n    type    = \"node\",\n    id      = node_ids,\n    lat     = coords[seq_len(n_unique), \"Y\"],\n    lon     = coords[seq_len(n_unique), \"X\"],\n    stringsAsFactors = FALSE\n  )\n  nodes$tags <- vector(\"list\", n_unique)\n  nodes$members <- vector(\"list\", n_unique)\n  \n  # Way row (closed: first node repeated at end)\n  way_df <- data.frame(type = \"way\", id = way_id, lat = NA_real_, lon = NA_real_,\n                       stringsAsFactors = FALSE)\n  way_df$tags    <- list(data.frame(key = character(), value = character()))\n  way_df$members <- list(matrix(\n    c(rep(\"node\", n_unique + 1L),\n      as.character(c(node_ids, node_ids[1L]))),\n    ncol = 2, dimnames = list(NULL, c(\"type\", \"ref\"))\n  ))\n  \n  # Relation tags (avoid column name clash with \"type\" by using \"boundary_type\")\n  rel_tags <- data.frame(\n    key = c(\"type\", \"boundary\", \"admin_level\", \"name\",\n            \"is_in:country\", \"is_in:state\", \"is_in:province\", \"wikidata\"),\n    value = c(\"boundary\", \"administrative\", \"8\",\n              paste0(\"Municipio \", row$adm3_name),\n              \"Bolivia\", row$adm1_name, row$adm2_name,\n              row$qid),\n    stringsAsFactors = FALSE\n  )\n  \n  rel_df <- data.frame(type = \"relation\", id = rel_id, lat = NA_real_, lon = NA_real_,\n                       stringsAsFactors = FALSE)\n  rel_df$tags    <- list(rel_tags)\n  rel_df$members <- list(matrix(\n    c(\"way\", as.character(way_id), \"outer\"),\n    nrow = 1, ncol = 3, dimnames = list(NULL, c(\"type\", \"ref\", \"role\"))\n  ))\n  \n  list(\n    objects  = rbind(nodes, way_df, rel_df),\n    next_id  = rel_id - 1L\n  )\n}\n\n# Test on Tito Yupanqui (smallest)\ntest_row <- shapes_ready[6, ]\nresult <- sf_polygon_to_osm_objects(test_row)\nstr(result$objects, max.level = 1)\ncat(\"Objects created:\", nrow(result$objects), \"(nodes + 1 way + 1 relation)\\n\")","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01AYtGXxyEW21msNCnFAxSpK"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# Build all 6 municipality objects in sequence, with non-overlapping negative IDs\nall_obje...[truncated, total 611 chars]","intent":"Build all 6 municipalities as OSM objects"},"intent":"Build all 6 municipalities as OSM objects","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Build all 6 municipality objects in sequence, with non-overlapping negative IDs\nall_objects_list <- list()\nid_cursor <- -1L\n\nfor (i in seq_len(nrow(shapes_ready))) {\n  row <- shapes_ready[i, ]\n  result <- sf_polygon_to_osm_objects(row, id_start = id_cursor)\n  all_objects_list[[i]] <- result$objects\n  id_cursor <- result$next_id\n}\n\nall_objects <- do.call(rbind, all_objects_list)\ncat(\"Total OSM objects to create:\", nrow(all_objects), \"\\n\")\ncat(\"  nodes:\", sum(all_objects$type == \"node\"), \"\\n\")\ncat(\"  ways: \", sum(all_objects$type == \"way\"), \"\\n\")\ncat(\"  rels: \", sum(all_objects$type == \"relation\"), \"\\n\")","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

If missing, it's assumed that ‘tags’\n          column contain the tags (see details).\n\nkeep_na_tags: If ‘TRUE’, don't drop the empty tags specified in\n          ‘tag_columns’ and add ‘NA’ as a value. Useful to remove\n          specific tags with ‘osmchange_modify()’ and specific\n          ‘tag_keys’.\n\n_\bV_\ba_\bl_\bu_\be:\n\n     An ‘osmapi_objects’\n\n_\bS_\be_\be _\bA_\bl_\bs_\bo:\n\n     Other get OSM objects' functions: ‘osm_bbox_objects()’,\n     ‘osm_get_objects()’, ‘osm_history_object()’,\n     ‘osm_relations_object()’, ‘osm_ways_node()’\n\n_\bE_\bx_\ba_\bm_\bp_\bl_\be_\bs:\n\n     x <- data.frame(\n       type = c(\"node\", \"node\", \"way\"), id = 1:3, name = c(NA, NA, \"My way\")\n     )\n     x$members <- list(NULL, NULL, 1:2)\n     obj <- osmapi_objects(x, tag_columns = \"name\")\n     obj"},"elapsedMs":287}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01EKWVo2ET8VJEdoVfoMYfEa"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# The issue is that osmapi_objects expects tags as either a 'tags' list-column \n# (list of...[truncated, total 981 chars]","intent":"Fix tag format for osmapi_objects"},"intent":"Fix tag format for osmapi_objects","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# The issue is that osmapi_objects expects tags as either a 'tags' list-column \n# (list of data.frames with key/value) OR named tag columns.\n# My data already has a 'tags' list-column — let me pass tag_columns = character(0)\n# but check the format of nodes where tags = NULL\n\n# Nodes have NULL in their tags list — need to be empty data.frames instead\nall_objects2 <- all_objects\nfor (i in which(all_objects2$type == \"node\")) {\n  all_objects2$tags[[i]] <- data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0))\n}\nfor (i in which(all_objects2$type == \"way\")) {\n  all_objects2$tags[[i]] <- data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0))\n}\n\n# Also members for nodes should be NULL not list(NULL)\nfor (i in which(all_objects2$type == \"node\")) {\n  all_objects2$members[[i]] <- NULL\n}\n\nosm_obj <- osmapi_objects(all_objects2, tag_columns = character(0))\ncat(\"osmapi_objects created, rows:\", nrow(osm_obj), \"\\n\")\nosmcha <- osmchange_create(osm_obj)\ncat(\"osmchange document created\\n\")","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01EKWVo2ET8VJEdoVfoMYfEa"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\nError in `$<-.data.frame`(`*tmp*`, \"members\", value = list(NULL, NULL,  : \n  repla...[truncated, total 219 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"Error in `$<-.data.frame`(`*tmp*`, \"members\", value = list(NULL, NULL,  : \n  replacement has 3815 rows, data has 3816\nError in expression 4 of 8: replacement has 3815 rows, data has 3816"},"elapsedMs":2938}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_01FvJmsYNDTptR8xwtRea3Zv"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# Fix the loop — all_objects has 3816 rows, need to set nodes (3804) and ways (6)\ncat(\"nod...[truncated, total 872 chars]","intent":"Fix member assignment with correct indexing"},"intent":"Fix member assignment with correct indexing","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Fix the loop — all_objects has 3816 rows, need to set nodes (3804) and ways (6)\ncat(\"node indices range:\", range(which(all_objects2$type == \"node\")), \"\\n\")\ncat(\"way indices range:\", range(which(all_objects2$type == \"way\")), \"\\n\")\n\n# Set members for nodes to NULL properly using lapply\nnode_idx <- which(all_objects2$type == \"node\")\nway_idx  <- which(all_objects2$type == \"way\")\n\nall_objects2$tags[node_idx] <- lapply(node_idx, function(i)\n  data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0)))\nall_objects2$tags[way_idx] <- lapply(way_idx, function(i)\n  data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0)))\nall_objects2$members[node_idx] <- vector(\"list\", length(node_idx))\n\nosm_obj <- osmapi_objects(all_objects2, tag_columns = character(0))\ncat(\"osmapi_objects created:\", nrow(osm_obj), \"rows\\n\")\nosmcha <- osmchange_create(osm_obj)\ncat(\"osmchange document ready\\n\")","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_015yda1s5fm4hPF1jrBemenR"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# Debug osmchange_create error — check the example from the vignette\nd <- data.frame(\n  ty...[truncated, total 499 chars]","intent":"Test osmchange_create with vignette example"},"intent":"Test osmchange_create with vignette example","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Debug osmchange_create error — check the example from the vignette\nd <- data.frame(\n  type = c(\"node\", \"node\", \"way\", \"relation\"),\n  id = -(1:4),\n  lat = c(0, 1, NA, NA),\n  lon = c(0, 1, NA, NA),\n  name = c(NA, NA, \"My way\", \"Our relation\")\n)\nd$members <- list(\n  NULL, NULL, -(1:2),\n  matrix(c(\"node\", \"-1\", \"node\", \"-2\", \"way\", \"-3\"),\n         nrow = 3, ncol = 2, byrow = TRUE, dimnames = list(NULL, c(\"type\", \"ref\")))\n)\nobj_ex <- osmapi_objects(d, tag_columns = \"name\")\nosmchange_create(obj_ex)","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_015yda1s5fm4hPF1jrBemenR"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\n  action_type     type id  lat  lon                             members\n1      cre...[truncated, total 526 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"action_type     type id  lat  lon                             members\n1      create     node -1    0    0                                NULL\n2      create     node -2    1    1                                NULL\n3      create      way -3 <NA> <NA>                     2 nodes: -1, -2\n4      create relation -4 <NA> <NA> 3 members: node/-1, node/-2, way/-3\n                      tags\n1                  No tags\n2                  No tags\n3       1 tag: name=My way\n4 1 tag: name=Our relation"},"elapsedMs":388}</script></tool-result></div>



<div><tool-call id="toolu_017DGEEG3AAjSE9PSedY3Rua"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"# Key insight from vignette: members for ways must be a plain integer/character VECTOR,\n# ...[truncated, total 2574 chars]","intent":"Fix way members format and rebuild all objects"},"intent":"Fix way members format and rebuild all objects","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"# Key insight from vignette: members for ways must be a plain integer/character VECTOR,\n# not a matrix. Only relations use a matrix with type/ref/role columns.\n# Let me fix the way members format.\n\nsf_polygon_to_osm_objects <- function(row, id_start = -1L) {\n  coords    <- st_coordinates(row$geometry)[, c(\"X\", \"Y\")]\n  n_unique  <- nrow(coords) - 1L\n  node_ids  <- seq(id_start, id_start - n_unique + 1L, by = -1L)\n  way_id    <- id_start - n_unique\n  rel_id    <- id_start - n_unique - 1L\n  \n  # Nodes\n  nodes <- data.frame(\n    type = \"node\", id = node_ids,\n    lat  = coords[seq_len(n_unique), \"Y\"],\n    lon  = coords[seq_len(n_unique), \"X\"],\n    stringsAsFactors = FALSE\n  )\n  nodes$tags    <- replicate(n_unique, data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0)), simplify = FALSE)\n  nodes$members <- vector(\"list\", n_unique)  # all NULL\n  \n  # Way — members is a character vector of node IDs (closed ring)\n  way_df <- data.frame(type = \"way\", id = way_id, lat = NA_real_, lon = NA_real_,\n                       stringsAsFactors = FALSE)\n  way_df$tags    <- list(data.frame(key = character(0), value = character(0)))\n  way_df$members <- list(as.character(c(node_ids, node_ids[1L])))\n  \n  # Relation — members is a 3-col matrix with type/ref/role\n  rel_tags <- data.frame(\n    key = c(\"type\", \"boundary\", \"admin_level\", \"name\",\n            \"is_in:country\", \"is_in:state\", \"is_in:province\", \"wikidata\"),\n    value = c(\"boundary\", \"administrative\", \"8\",\n              paste0(\"Municipio \", row$adm3_name),\n              \"Bolivia\", row$adm1_name, row$adm2_name, row$qid),\n    stringsAsFactors = FALSE\n  )\n  rel_df <- data.frame(type = \"relation\", id = rel_id, lat = NA_real_, lon = NA_real_,\n                       stringsAsFactors = FALSE)\n  rel_df$tags    <- list(rel_tags)\n  rel_df$members <- list(matrix(\n    c(\"way\", as.character(way_id), \"outer\"),\n    nrow = 1, ncol = 3, dimnames = list(NULL, c(\"type\", \"ref\", \"role\"))\n  ))\n  \n  list(objects = rbind(nodes, way_df, rel_df), next_id = rel_id - 1L)\n}\n\n# Rebuild all objects\nall_objects_list <- list()\nid_cursor <- -1L\nfor (i in seq_len(nrow(shapes_ready))) {\n  result <- sf_polygon_to_osm_objects(shapes_ready[i, ], id_start = id_cursor)\n  all_objects_list[[i]] <- result$objects\n  id_cursor <- result$next_id\n}\nall_objects <- do.call(rbind, all_objects_list)\n\nosm_obj <- osmapi_objects(all_objects, tag_columns = character(0))\nosmcha  <- osmchange_create(osm_obj)\ncat(\"osmchange ready:\", nrow(osmcha), \"objects\\n\")\n# Preview relation rows\nosmcha |> filter(type == \"relation\") |> select(action_type, type, id, tags)","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01R5HbtC4FoFYWtyy3cfk1Tr"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"content","value":[{"type":"text","text":"Output:\nA single object matching ‘osmchange_create’ was found\nIt was found in the followin...[truncated, total 987 chars]"}]},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"A single object matching ‘osmchange_create’ was found\nIt was found in the following places\n  package:osmapiR\n  namespace:osmapiR\nwith value\n\nfunction (x, format = c(\"R\", \"osc\", \"xml\")) \n{\n    format <- match.arg(format)\n    stopifnot(inherits(x, \"osmapi_objects\"))\n    if (nrow(x) == 0) {\n        return(osmchange_create_empty(format = format))\n    }\n    if (inherits(x, \"tags_wide\")) {\n        x <- tags_wide2list(x)\n    }\n    if (!\"id\" %in% names(x)) {\n        x$id <- -seq_len(nrow(x))\n    }\n    x_type <- split(x, x$type)\n    osmchange <- do.call(rbind, x_type[c(\"node\", \"way\", \"relation\")])\n    rownames(osmchange) <- NULL\n    osmchange <- cbind(action_type = \"create\", osmchange)\n    if (format == \"R\") {\n        class(osmchange) <- c(\"osmapi_OsmChange\", \"osmapi_objects\", \n            \"data.frame\")\n    }\n    else {\n        osmchange <- osmcha_DF2xml(osmchange)\n    }\n    return(osmchange)\n}\n<bytecode: 0x10ad73ee0>\n<environment: namespace:osmapiR>"},"elapsedMs":726}</script></tool-result></div>

The issue is that with\n# tag_columns = character(0) it's treating ALL columns as tags or dropping them.\n# \n# Looking at the vignette example: it passes tag_columns = \"name\" and \n# the required structural cols (type, id, lat, lon, members) are kept automatically.\n# With tag_columns = character(0), it seems the structural cols are also dropped.\n# \n# Solution: pass the pre-built tags column in a different way.\n# The vignette shows we should pass tag_columns for named tag columns,\n# and have separate list-column named \"tags\" for pre-built tags.\n# Let me try without calling osmapi_objects and use the df directly.\n\n# Check if osmchange_create accepts a raw data.frame\ntryCatch(\n  osmchange_create(one_obj),\n  error = function(e) cat(\"Error:\", conditionMessage(e), \"\\n\")\n)","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<span class="thinking-topic">Understanding the real goal</span>



The user doesn't want to figure out osmapiR — they want files they can use to upload the shapes to OSM directly. The standard format for importing into OSM is `.osm` XML format, which can be loaded in JOSM (Java OpenStreetMap Editor) and then uploaded.



<span class="thinking-topic">Choosing the right output format</span>



The best approach is to generate `.osm` XML files for each municipality that can be opened in JOSM and uploaded. JOSM is the standard tool for this kind of batch import.

The `.osm` format looks like:
Writing OSM XML structure...
</thinking><span data-thinking-duration-ms="8810"></span></div>

I'll generate `.osm` XML files — one per municipality — that can be opened directly in JOSM and uploaded.

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01CQkeYdzWGbfQLCHtLNWjNL"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"library(sf)\nlibrary(dplyr)\n\n# Load shapes (already simplified to < 1900 nodes/way)\nadmin3 ...[truncated, total 1275 chars]","intent":"Load and prepare shapes"},"intent":"Load and prepare shapes","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"library(sf)\nlibrary(dplyr)\n\n# Load shapes (already simplified to < 1900 nodes/way)\nadmin3 <- st_read(\n  \"../ultimate-consequences/maps/humdata.org_cod-ab-bol/bol_admin_boundaries.shp\",\n  layer = \"bol_admin3\", quiet = TRUE\n)\n\nnot_in_osm_df <- tribble(\n  ~qid,          ~ine_code, ~label_en,\n  \"Q1552084\",    \"021703\",  \"Tito Yupanqui\",\n  \"Q1544578\",    \"020307\",  \"Nazacara de Pacajes\",\n  \"Q775675\",     \"050204\",  \"Chuquihuta\",\n  \"Q1147210\",    \"051103\",  \"Ckochas\",\n  \"Q520394\",     \"021701\",  \"Copacabana\",\n  \"Q1819398\",    \"021702\",  \"San Pedro de Tiquina\"\n  # Q106409795 San Pedro de Macha not in shapefile\n)\n\nsimplify_to_way_limit <- function(geom, max_nodes = 1900) {\n  n <- nrow(st_coordinates(geom))\n  if (n <= max_nodes) return(geom)\n  tol <- 0.001\n  repeat {\n    simp <- st_simplify(geom, dTolerance = tol, preserveTopology = TRUE)\n    if (nrow(st_coordinates(simp)) <= max_nodes) return(simp)\n    tol <- tol * 2\n  }\n}\n\nshapes_ready <- admin3 |>\n  mutate(ine_code = substr(adm3_pcode, 3, 8)) |>\n  inner_join(not_in_osm_df, by = \"ine_code\") |>\n  mutate(geometry = purrr::map(geometry, simplify_to_way_limit) |> st_sfc(crs = 4326)) |>\n  select(qid, ine_code, label_en, adm3_name, adm1_name, adm2_name, geometry)\n\ncat(\"Municipalities ready:\", nrow(shapes_ready), \"\\n\")","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01U62dwtLXJkPt5CyDjgx7g3"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"name":"executeCode","input":{"code":"write_osm_file <- function(row, outdir = \"output/osm_imports\") {\n  dir.create(outdir, show...[truncated, total 2145 chars]","intent":"Generate OSM XML files for each municipality"},"intent":"Generate OSM XML files for each municipality","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"write_osm_file <- function(row, outdir = \"output/osm_imports\") {\n  dir.create(outdir, showWarnings = FALSE, recursive = TRUE)\n  \n  coords   <- st_coordinates(row$geometry)[, c(\"X\", \"Y\")]\n  n_unique <- nrow(coords) - 1L  # last coord = first (closed ring)\n  node_ids <- -(seq_len(n_unique))\n  way_id   <- -(n_unique + 1L)\n  rel_id   <- -(n_unique + 2L)\n  \n  # Helper: escape XML special chars\n  xe <- function(s) gsub(\"&\", \"&amp;\",\n          gsub(\"<\", \"&lt;\",\n          gsub(\">\", \"&gt;\",\n          gsub('\"', \"&quot;\", s))))\n\n  lines <- c(\n    '<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>',\n    '<osm version=\"0.6\">',\n    ''\n  )\n\n  # Nodes\n  for (i in seq_len(n_unique)) {\n    lines <- c(lines, sprintf(\n      '  <node id=\"%d\" action=\"create\" lat=\"%.7f\" lon=\"%.7f\" />',\n      node_ids[i], coords[i, \"Y\"], coords[i, \"X\"]\n    ))\n  }\n  lines <- c(lines, '')\n\n  # Way (closed ring)\n  lines <- c(lines, sprintf('  <way id=\"%d\" action=\"create\">', way_id))\n  for (nid in c(node_ids, node_ids[1])) {\n    lines <- c(lines, sprintf('    <nd ref=\"%d\"/>', nid))\n  }\n  lines <- c(lines, '  </way>', '')\n\n  # Relation\n  lines <- c(lines, sprintf('  <relation id=\"%d\" action=\"create\">', rel_id))\n  lines <- c(lines, sprintf('    <member type=\"way\" ref=\"%d\" role=\"outer\"/>', way_id))\n  \n  tags <- list(\n    type            = \"boundary\",\n    boundary        = \"administrative\",\n    admin_level     = \"8\",\n    name            = paste0(\"Municipio \", xe(row$adm3_name)),\n    `is_in:country` = \"Bolivia\",\n    `is_in:state`   = xe(row$adm1_name),\n    `is_in:province`= xe(row$adm2_name),\n    wikidata        = row$qid,\n    source          = \"Instituto Nacional de Estadistica - Bolivia (humdata.org)\"\n  )\n  \n  for (nm in names(tags)) {\n    lines <- c(lines, sprintf('    <tag k=\"%s\" v=\"%s\"/>', nm, tags[[nm]]))\n  }\n  lines <- c(lines, '  </relation>', '', '</osm>')\n  \n  slug    <- gsub(\"[^A-Za-z0-9]\", \"_\", row$label_en)\n  outfile <- file.path(outdir, paste0(row$ine_code, \"_\", slug, \".osm\"))\n  writeLines(lines, outfile)\n  cat(\"Written:\", outfile, \"\\n\")\n  outfile\n}\n\nfiles <- purrr::map_chr(seq_len(nrow(shapes_ready)), function(i) {\n  write_osm_file(shapes_ready[i, ])\n})","language":"r"}}</script></tool-call></div>

**To upload in JOSM:**
1. File → Open each `.osm` file
2. Verify the boundary looks correct on the map
3. Upload (Ctrl+Shift+Up) with a changeset comment like `Add missing Bolivian municipal boundary (admin_level=8) from INE/humdata.org`

Each relation is tagged `boundary=administrative`, `admin_level=8`, `name=Municipio …`, `wikidata=Q…`, `is_in:state`, `is_in:province`.
